========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Before the house thing? No, I don’t think there was another time like that that I can really point to. Not in my earlier life, anyway. I mean, I’ve had good moods before — everybody has good stretches, you know, where the weather’s nice or the family’s doing all right and you feel lighter. But not that same kind of… charged-up feeling.

During the house episode, yes, most days I felt almost joyful, like I was young again, like there was this current running through me and I could just keep going. It wasn’t just “I’m in a decent mood.” It felt bigger than that, like everything had meaning and I was part of something important.

But it could turn quick, too. If my wife questioned what I was doing, or if contractors acted like they didn’t understand or pushed back on me, I’d get hot fast. I’d go from feeling excited and full of purpose to yelling before I even realized how loud I was getting. At the time I felt like they were blocking something important, so I didn’t see it as me being unreasonable. Looking back, I can see I was on a hair trigger.

But before that period with the house, no… I can’t remember several days of being unusually high like that.

And I kept working after dark because it felt important—like if I stopped, even for one night, the house and the whole block would miss its chance.\n\nI know that probably sounds strange the way I’m saying it, but at the time it didn’t feel like wasting money or pushing too hard. It felt like something that had to be finished. They’re upset about the savings, and I guess that’s what brought me in.\n\nDoctor: While you were working on the house, did you hear or see things that other people said they did not?\n\nPatient: I don’t know exactly what you mean by that. It was more like… the house had been chosen, or the neighborhood had been picked, and I was supposed to do this one last big civic art piece before I’m gone or too old to do anything that matters.\n\nPeople think I’m mixed up about it, but I’m telling you, there were signs. Trucks would slow down in front of the house, not just once, but enough times that I noticed it. Neighbors would look over a certain way, like they knew, even if they wouldn’t say it out loud. Little things like that kept lining up, and to me it proved they understood there was something bigger happening there.\n\nSo yes, I believed it had a mission. Maybe other people didn’t want to admit it, or maybe they couldn’t understand it the way I did, but I felt sure the house and that whole block were part of something important.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least several days when you felt very depressed or down most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d put it that way. I mean, I’ve had bad days, sure—everybody my age has bad days, days where you sit and think too much or you don’t feel like dealing with people. But “most of the day, nearly every day,” for several days… I’m not sure.\n\nI wouldn’t want to say yes if I don’t really mean it. Sometimes I get worn down or quiet, especially when people are on me about something, but I don’t keep track of it like that. I haven’t really thought of myself as being down like that for days on end. It’s more… I don’t know, I have moods like anybody, but I can’t give you a clear “yes” on that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had those kinds of strong beliefs when you were not feeling unusually energized or driven?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. Not in that same way.\n\nBefore I got all charged up about the house—before I had the plans in my head and felt like I had to get it done, like it was this big thing I was meant to do—I wasn’t walking around seeing messages in every little thing. I mean, trucks went by before, neighbors looked over before, that’s just what happens on a street. But I didn’t take it as meaning something special then.\n\nIt was only once I got really driven with the remodeling, once I started feeling like the house was going to be this big civic project, this important thing for the block, that those looks and trucks slowing down started feeling like signs. That’s when it all seemed connected. Before that, I don’t remember thinking the house had that kind of meaning.\n\nDoctor: During that time with the house, did you need less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that cleanly. I know sleep got… odd around then, but I wasn’t keeping track like, “I slept four hours” or “I slept eight hours.” I didn’t write it down or anything.\n\nWhen I was caught up with the house, I’d be up thinking about things, going over what needed to be done, and sometimes it felt like nighttime was just another part of the day. But whether I actually needed less sleep, or whether I just wasn’t sleeping the same because my mind was busy, I don’t know.\n\nI remember people acting like I wasn’t resting enough, but people say things when they’re already worried about you. From my side, I can’t give you a clear yes or no. It wasn’t something I noticed in a simple way at the time.\n\nDoctor: Did your family bring you to the hospital because they felt you were unable to take care of yourself or were putting yourself or others at risk?\n\nPatient: That’s what they say, yes. I mean, I don’t think of it as me being unable to take care of myself exactly, because from my point of view I was taking care of something important. But my family says that’s why they brought me in.\n\nThey were upset because I used a lot of our savings on wood and lights and display things for the house, and I kept pushing the work even after dark. They kept telling me to stop, that it was too much, that I was going to ruin us or get hurt or cause trouble. But at the time it didn’t feel reckless to me. It felt like if I stopped, the whole house would miss its chance, and not just the house—the block, too. Like there was this moment and I had to see it through.\n\nSo yes, they brought me here because they thought I was going too far and not thinking about the consequences. I understood they were scared, but I didn’t feel like they understood what I was trying to do.\n\nDoctor: During that time with the house, were you talking much more than usual?\n\nPatient: Yes… I guess I was, if I’m being honest. At the time I didn’t think of it as “talking too much,” you know, I thought I was just explaining what needed to happen with the house. But once I got going, especially if somebody asked me why I was doing a certain thing or why we needed this or that, I’d start laying it all out and then the words would just keep coming.\n\nMy wife would say, “You’re not letting me talk,” or “You’re running right over me,” and I’d feel like I had to finish the whole thought before she jumped in, because it all connected in my head. I’d be trying to explain the plans, the order of the work, what it was supposed to become, and I guess I didn’t leave much room for her to get a sentence in. I wasn’t trying to be rude, but yeah, I was talking a lot more than usual when I started explaining those plans.\n\nDoctor: During that time with the house, did you ever feel that other people were trying to send you special messages?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean… I wouldn’t have called them “messages” at first, but yes, that’s what it felt like.\n\nIt was like the neighborhood was reacting to what I was doing with the house. People think I got mixed up about it, I know that, but I’m telling you, it didn’t feel random. A truck would slow down in front of the house, not stop exactly, just slow, like the driver was taking it in or making sure I saw that he saw it. Neighbors would look over from their porches or pause a little longer than usual when they walked by. Even small things like that felt connected, like they knew this wasn’t just me decorating or fixing boards.\n\nI felt sure the neighborhood had been picked for something, for my last big civic art piece, if that makes sense. Like this was the place it was supposed to happen, and people around me understood it even if they weren’t saying it out loud. So when I saw those looks, or a vehicle slowing, or somebody standing across the street watching, I took it as proof they knew too. Not like a letter in the mail, but signs. Little confirmations.\n\nDoctor: Before your family brought you in, did you feel you needed to be in the hospital?\n\nPatient: No… I didn’t feel like I needed to be in the hospital. Not before they brought me in.\n\nI know that probably sounds like I wasn’t seeing things the way they were seeing them, but from where I was standing, I felt busy, not sick. I felt like there was something I was in the middle of, something important, and being taken away from it felt like everybody was overreacting or interrupting me right when I needed to keep going.\n\nI understood they were worried, at least on some level. I could see it on their faces and hear it in how they talked to me. But I didn’t think, “I need to go to the hospital.” I thought, “They don’t understand what I’m doing.” That was more how it felt to me.\n\nDoctor: After your energy and drive about the house settled down, did those beliefs that the neighborhood was sending you signs continue?\n\nPatient: No… not really in the same way. Once that drive settled down — once I wasn’t so wound up about turning the house into this big thing, this civic piece or whatever I thought it was — those signs didn’t keep carrying the same meaning.\n\nBefore all that started, I wasn’t walking around thinking trucks slowing down meant something, or that a neighbor looking over had some special purpose behind it. I mean, people look at houses, trucks go by, that’s normal. It was only after I got so charged up with the remodeling plans, when the whole house started feeling like it was supposed to become something important, that I began reading those things as signs.\n\nSo after that energy came down, it was more like… I could see that those were just ordinary things again, or at least they didn’t hit me with that same certainty. The special meaning was tied up with that whole project in my head. It wasn’t something I’d been believing before, and it didn’t really continue once that feeling settled.\n\nDoctor: Before this episode with the house, had you ever had another time in your life when you felt unusually energized or driven like that?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to answer that. Not that I remember in a clear way, no.\n\nI mean, I’ve had times in my life where I got interested in something and put a lot of effort into it, like anyone does. A project around the house, or an idea I’d chew on for a while. But if you mean like this — where it felt that charged, like I had to keep going and everything was wrapped up in it — I can’t say I remember another time like that.\n\nAt my age, you think back and everything kind of blends together some. There were busy times, stressful times, times I probably pushed myself too much. But nothing is coming to mind that felt the same as what happened with the house.\n\nDoctor: Before this house episode, was there ever another time in your life when you felt unusually energized or driven in a similar way?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can say yes to that. I mean, I’ve had times where I got caught up in things, sure — everybody does, especially over a lifetime. You get an idea in your head, you work on something, you stay busy, maybe you push harder than you should. But if you mean the same kind of feeling as with the house, where it felt like I was being pulled along by it and couldn’t just set it down… I don’t remember another time like that.\n\nIt’s hard for me to separate normal busy times from something more than that, looking back now. There were years where I had responsibilities, things to fix, things to figure out, and maybe I had more energy some days than others. But nothing stands out to me like, “Yes, that was the same thing.” This house situation felt different enough that it’s the one everybody noticed, and it’s the one I remember being questioned about like this.\n\nSo… no, not that I can clearly point to. If it happened before, I don’t have a clear memory of it in the way you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Before the house episode, had you ever had a period lasting at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can give you a clean yes-or-no on that. I mean, I’ve had low times, of course. At 73, you don’t get through life without stretches where things feel heavy or you’re not yourself, and looking back it all gets a little muddy.\n\nBut if you mean a definite two weeks where I was depressed most of the day nearly every day, before the house thing… I don’t know that I can point to one and say, yes, that happened exactly like that. Nothing is coming to mind in a clear way. There were times I was down or discouraged, sure, but I don’t remember tracking it like that, day after day, or thinking of it as a spell that lasted a certain amount of time.\n\nSo I’d have to say I’m not really sure. I can’t honestly tell you there was a clear period like that before this.\n\nDoctor: At that time, were you certain you had a special role or mission that ordinary people did not have?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I know how that sounds when I say it out loud, and I know people think I got mixed up about it. But at the time, no, it wasn’t just me having some ordinary idea. I felt sure there was a reason I was the one seeing it and doing it.\n\nIt was like the neighborhood had been chosen, or set apart somehow, for this last big civic art piece I was supposed to bring together. Not just a little yard project or something to keep busy with — it felt bigger than that, like it had a purpose for everybody around there, and I had a part in it that other people didn’t understand.\n\nAnd little things would happen that, to me, weren’t little. A truck would slow down out front, or a neighbor would look over a certain way, and I’d think, see, they know. They’re aware of it. They may not come right out and say it, but they know this is happening. I took those things as signs, I guess, but at the time I didn’t think of them as “signs.” I thought they were proof.\n\nSo yes, I did feel like I had a special role. I still have trouble just dismissing it as nothing, because it felt very clear to me then.\n\nDoctor: Before the house episode, had there ever been another time in your life when you needed much less sleep than usual and still felt full of energy?\n\nPatient: I don’t really remember a time like that before the house. I mean, I’ve had plenty of nights in my life where I didn’t sleep well — you get older, or you’ve got things on your mind, or you wake up too early and can’t get back to sleep. That’s happened here and there. But that’s different from needing less sleep and still feeling full of energy, like it didn’t matter.\n\nBefore that episode, I can’t point to some period and say, “Yes, I was sleeping only a little and felt great anyway.” Nothing clear comes to mind. At least not in a way that stands out to me now.\n\nDoctor: During the time with the house, were you still going to work or handling your usual daily responsibilities the way you normally would?\n\nPatient: No, not really the way I normally would. I mean, I’m retired, so it wasn’t like I was missing a job or not showing up somewhere every morning, but the usual things at home… yes, those got pretty badly affected.\n\nThe house itself became the main thing. My materials and the things I was putting together, or half-putting together, started taking over rooms we actually need to live in. It wasn’t just one corner of the garage or a little table somewhere. It spread into the house, and then my wife and I were trying to get around all of it, and it got hard to use parts of the house like normal. I can see that now more than I could at the time.\n\nAnd there were arguments. I didn’t handle people questioning me very well, and some people I care about started keeping their distance. They didn’t want to come by, or they avoided talking about it, or avoided me altogether after things got heated. So no, I can’t honestly say I was keeping up with life as usual. I thought I was doing something important, but it did make daily living harder for both me and my wife.\n\nDoctor: Before the house episode, had there ever been another time in your life when you felt unusually happy or high for several days?\n\nPatient: Before the house thing? No, I don’t think there was another time like that that I can really point to. Not in my earlier life, anyway. I mean, I’ve had good moods before — everybody has good stretches, you know, where the weather’s nice or the family’s doing all right and you feel lighter. But not that same kind of… charged-up feeling.\n\nDuring the house episode, yes, most days I felt almost joyful, like I was young again, like there was this current running through me and I could just keep going. It wasn’t just “I’m in a decent mood.” It felt bigger than that, like everything had meaning and I was part of something important.\n\nBut it could turn quick, too. If my wife questioned what I was doing, or if contractors acted like they didn’t understand or pushed back on me, I’d get hot fast. I’d go from feeling excited and full of purpose to yelling before I even realized how loud I was getting. At the time I felt like they were blocking something important, so I didn’t see it as me being unreasonable. Looking back, I can see I was on a hair trigger.\n\nBut before that period with the house, no… I can’t remember several days of being unusually high like that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No… not that I can separate out, anyway. I don’t remember sitting around feeling normal, just my regular self, and then suddenly thinking trucks going by meant something or that the neighbors’ faces were giving me some kind of message.

That all seemed to come in once I got really wound up about the house and the remodeling plans. I was already feeling driven, like the house wasn’t just a house anymore — like it was supposed to be something bigger, almost like a project for the community, like I had to get it right. And then after that, I started noticing things and reading into them. A truck passing at a certain time, or the way somebody looked over at me, or didn’t look at me… it started to feel connected.

But before I got that charged up, no, I wasn’t walking around seeing signs in ordinary things like that. It came along with that whole push about turning the house into this big civic project, not during a calm, normal period.

And I kept working after dark because it felt important—like if I stopped, even for one night, the house and the whole block would miss its chance.\n\nI know that probably sounds strange the way I’m saying it, but at the time it didn’t feel like wasting money or pushing too hard. It felt like something that had to be finished. They’re upset about the savings, and I guess that’s what brought me in.\n\nDoctor: While you were working on the house, did you hear or see things that other people said they did not?\n\nPatient: I don’t know exactly what you mean by that. I mean, I was out there a lot, late at night, and when you’re tired and it’s quiet, you hear all kinds of things—wind in the trees, boards settling, cars going by, little noises from the neighborhood. But I can’t say there was something like… some person standing there that nobody else could see, or a voice talking to me plain as day.\n\nPeople kept saying I was acting like the house was more important than anything, and maybe I was wrapped up in it, sure. But as far as hearing or seeing things other people didn’t, I’m not sure. Nobody was out there with me the whole time to compare it to, you know? It was more like… the house had been chosen, or the neighborhood had been picked, and I was supposed to do this one last big civic art piece before I’m gone or too old to do anything that matters.\n\nPeople think I’m mixed up about it, but I’m telling you, there were signs. But my family says that’s why they brought me in.\n\nThey were upset because I used a lot of our savings on wood and lights and display things for the house, and I kept pushing the work even after dark. They kept telling me to stop, that it was too much, that I was going to ruin us or get hurt or cause trouble. But at the time it didn’t feel reckless to me. It felt like if I stopped, the whole house would miss its chance, and not just the house—the block, too. Like there was this moment and I had to see it through.\n\nSo yes, they brought me here because they thought I was going too far and not thinking about the consequences. I understood they were scared, but I didn’t feel like they understood what I was trying to do.\n\nDoctor: During that time with the house, were you talking much more than usual?\n\nPatient: Yes… I guess I was, if I’m being honest. At the time I didn’t think of it as “talking too much,” you know, I thought I was just explaining what needed to happen with the house. But once I got going, especially if somebody asked me why I was doing a certain thing or why we needed this or that, I’d start laying it all out and then the words would just keep coming.\n\nMy wife would say, “You’re not letting me talk,” or “You’re running right over me,” and I’d feel like I had to finish the whole thought before she jumped in, because it all connected in my head. I’d be trying to explain the plans, the order of the work, what it was supposed to become, and I guess I didn’t leave much room for her to get a sentence in. I wasn’t trying to be rude, but yeah, I was talking a lot more than usual when I started explaining those plans.\n\nDoctor: During that time with the house, did you ever feel that other people were trying to send you special messages?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean… I wouldn’t have called them “messages” at first, but yes, that’s what it felt like.\n\nIt was like the neighborhood was reacting to what I was doing with the house. People think I got mixed up about it, I know that, but I’m telling you, it didn’t feel random. A truck would slow down in front of the house, not stop exactly, just slow, like the driver was taking it in or making sure I saw that he saw it. Neighbors would look over from their porches or pause a little longer than usual when they walked by. Like this was the place it was supposed to happen, and people around me understood it even if they weren’t saying it out loud. Little confirmations.\n\nDoctor: Before your family brought you in, did you feel you needed to be in the hospital?\n\nPatient: No… I didn’t feel like I needed to be in the hospital. Not before they brought me in.\n\nI know that probably sounds like I wasn’t seeing things the way they were seeing them, but from where I was standing, I felt busy, not sick. I felt like there was something I was in the middle of, something important, and being taken away from it felt like everybody was overreacting or interrupting me right when I needed to keep going.\n\nI understood they were worried, at least on some level. I could see it on their faces and hear it in how they talked to me. But I didn’t think, “I need to go to the hospital.” I thought, “They don’t understand what I’m doing.” That was more how it felt to me.\n\nDoctor: After your energy and drive about the house settled down, did those beliefs that the neighborhood was sending you signs continue?\n\nPatient: No… not really in the same way. Once that drive settled down — once I wasn’t so wound up about turning the house into this big thing, this civic piece or whatever I thought it was — those signs didn’t keep carrying the same meaning.\n\nBefore all that started, I wasn’t walking around thinking trucks slowing down meant something, or that a neighbor looking over had some special purpose behind it. The special meaning was tied up with that whole project in my head. It wasn’t something I’d been believing before, and it didn’t really continue once that feeling settled.\n\nDoctor: Before this episode with the house, had you ever had another time in your life when you felt unusually energized or driven like that?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to answer that. I mean, I’ve had plenty of nights in my life where I didn’t sleep well — you get older, or you’ve got things on your mind, or you wake up too early and can’t get back to sleep. No, I don’t think there was another time like that that I can really point to. Not in my earlier life, anyway. I mean, I’ve had good moods before — everybody has good stretches, you know, where the weather’s nice or the family’s doing all right and you feel lighter. It wasn’t just “I’m in a decent mood.” It felt bigger than that, like everything had meaning and I was part of something important.\n\nBut it could turn quick, too. If my wife questioned what I was doing, or if contractors acted like they didn’t understand or pushed back on me, I’d get hot fast. I’d go from feeling excited and full of purpose to yelling before I even realized how loud I was getting. At the time I felt like they were blocking something important, so I didn’t see it as me being unreasonable. Looking back, I can see I was on a hair trigger.\n\nBut before that period with the house, no… I can’t remember several days of being unusually high like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had those kinds of special messages or fixed beliefs at a time when your mood and energy felt normal?\n\nPatient: No… not that I can separate out, anyway. I don’t remember sitting around feeling normal, just my regular self, and then suddenly thinking trucks going by meant something or that the neighbors’ faces were giving me some kind of message.\n\nThat all seemed to come in once I got really wound up about the house and the remodeling plans. I was already feeling driven, like the house wasn’t just a house anymore — like it was supposed to be something bigger, almost like a project for the community, like I had to get it right. And then after that, I started noticing things and reading into them. A truck passing at a certain time, or the way somebody looked over at me, or didn’t look at me… it started to feel connected.\n\nBut before I got that charged up, no, I wasn’t walking around seeing signs in ordinary things like that. It came along with that whole push about turning the house into this big civic project, not during a calm, normal period."
